This verifies initial target availability, not attack replay/control changes, actual Link procedure, broad battle resolution or native Omega. Counts unchanged 57 authored/34 absent; full audit open.

Venus Flytrap linked-destruction/shared-chain evidence (2026-10-01): fresh_terrarumian_venus_chain.cjs passes actual seeded Extra Monster Zone source pointing to Terrarumian ally; ignition targets/destroys linked ally and distinct second card. A fixture Quick-Play joins its chain; Venus draw Quick Effect is absent while chain is open and becomes available after chain ends. --no-chain-lock removes only flag condition in memory and fails forbidden Quick activation check. Candidate marker 7 is queried/asserted in engine. Public package 32-bit metadata writer places rscale at offset 48 (core link-marker slot), link_marker at 52; harness uses Link-only rscale-to-arrows transfer adapter, no database/production edits. Also maps pinned Omega CHAININFO_TARGET_CARDS to public flag 8, as existing Intervention fixture does. This is scoped geometry and one chain-direction/reset evidence, not actual Link procedure, protection, reverse-chain/all timing, targeting redirection, cross-copy HOPT or native Omega certification. Counts 57 authored/34 absent; full audit open.

CRITICAL Sylvan setcode correction (2026-10-01): inspecting pinned official Sagequoia c10530913 revealed its filters use 0x90, matching OFFICIAL_SHARED_SET_CODES['sylvan'] and candidate database rows. Added fresh_sylvan_metadata.cjs to load actual candidate card metadata for 238276572-575 (type, Level, race, attribute, ATK/DEF, alias, scales and setcodes). Omega setcode rows are little-endian BLOBs, not packed SQLite integers; the helper decodes both formats and independently asserts canonical 0x90. The first metadata helper attempt assumed integers and failed its assertion; it was corrected to decode the actual BLOB bytes before use. All four harnesses now replace fabricated production-card metadata with these real rows (including Greetings in Goleaf's integration case). Neutral allies/search cards and official seeded test metadata remain explicit fixtures. All 44 focused duels pass again: fifteen Flamushroomo, twelve Goleaf, ten Flamioak, seven Greetings. Greetings --old-setcode restores only wrong production/helper filters while actual staged metadata stays correct; optional addition and both official interoperability cases fail independently. Separate control trace preserved. Syntax passes. Earlier fixtures for other cards should likewise be compared with actual candidate metadata before full certification. Count stays 51/91 authored, 40 absent. No new official override in this correction; Sagequoia and remaining official paths still need integration/audit.

Sylvan Greetings groundwork (238276575), 2026-10-01: authored production Quick-Play Lua and three staged prompts. Its own activation branch has only syntax/staging evidence, NOT a duel pass. Added required shared dependency ccg_sylvan.lua because pinned Omega constants provide no excavation/confirmation event for Greetings' mandatory 'if excavated by a Sylvan effect, send it' instruction. The shared solver snapshots the excavated Group, separates Plant monsters and forced-send custom Spell/Trap IDs (Greetings/Intervention/Blast), excludes forced sends from optional Spell/Trap hand additions, sends with EFFECT+REVEAL, then orders only the remaining original cards. Flamushroomo/Goleaf/Flamioak now call it. Goleaf receives the original Plant Level independently of destination, preserving its 'also' behavior under Macro. Sorting one remaining card skips an unnecessary ordering prompt.

Integration evidence: fifteen Flamushroomo, eleven original Goleaf and ten Flamioak cases pass after adopting the helper. Added one actual Goleaf excavation of complete Greetings: Greetings is sent to GY with EFFECT/REVEAL, its own actual GY trigger recruits a Sylvan ally, and Goleaf's Level stays 3. Goleaf --send-gated-level control was updated to gate the helper's returned Level on the original card being in GY; it still fails Macro independently. Fixture setup only places the actual Greetings on top; no send/recruitment event is mocked. This does not verify Greetings' own five-card/add-two/Plant-lock activation. Current count: 51/91 authored, 40 absent.

Explicit unfinished compatibility requirement: official Sylvan scripts still perform their own Plant-only sending and do not use the shared solver. Thus Greetings/Intervention/Blast's mandatory excavation-send instruction is not yet implemented for every official Sylvan effect. Do not claim full intended behavior or certify Greetings until this is integrated/tested; do not disguise it as a public-engine gap. Next work: actual Greetings five-card activation, optional zero/one/two additions and forced self-send priority, Plant lock plus expiry, Sylvan versus unrelated effect-send recruitment, official Sylvan interoperability. The helper must accompany distributed production scripts; candidate staging alone is not distribution proof.

Sylvan Flamioak (238276574), 2026-10-01: authored complete production Lua and three prompts, syntax checked and candidate staged. Ten `fresh_sylvan_flamioak.cjs` cases pass: actual self-Summon from hand or GY responding to a Sylvan monster's Special Summon, followed by actual Sylvan Spell search from Deck/GY; opponent-field Sylvan Summon also permits self-recruitment because the printed condition is not player-limited; unrelated monster Summon rejects; full own field rejects despite the opponent's successful Sylvan Summon; two same-code copies share exactly one recruitment; choosing one, two or three on the actual AnnounceNumber prompt sends exactly that many Plant monsters with EFFECT+REVEAL reasons, and the same field copy cannot repeat its ignition that turn. --no-self-count independently fails the two-copy case. The whole production script is loaded with neutral cards and a real setup Spell that Summons or orders actual Deck cards. No engine/helper translation is used. Initial fixture failures were corrected by responding with AnnounceNumber.value and selecting the opponent's legal Monster Zone when the fixture Summons there; production was unchanged by those corrections.

Flamioak open coverage: Normal Summon search, mixed non-Plant excavation and chosen bottom ordering, short-Deck choices, hard search count sharing/reset, soft ignition count reset, hand/GY source removal before recruitment resolves, Necrovalley/Macro/locks, optional decline, negation, and native Omega. Current total: 50/91 originally missing scripts authored, 41 absent. Next absent script: Sylvan Greetings (238276575). These focused passes do not certify the full card or the full 711-card roster.

Sylvan Goleaf (238276573), 2026-10-01: authored production Lua and three candidate prompts; syntax checked/staged. `fresh_sylvan_goleaf.cjs` passes eleven full-script public-core cases: actual Plant excavation (EFFECT/REVEAL reasons) changes Level 3 to 6; non-Plant monster and Spell are actually moved to bottom and leave Level unchanged; actual Normal and Special Summon trigger selection/shuffling/placement of a Sylvan monster on Deck top, then its ignition excavates that exact card; official Macro Cosmos redirects the Plant into banishment while the independent 'also' Level change still applies; actual targeted GY revival of another Sylvan/self; ordinary-effect-send and deliberately REVEAL-tagged cost-send rejection; actual Quick-Play Spell chained after targeting banishes the target and prevents revival. Each ignition positive checks it cannot be activated twice on the same field copy in that turn. --no-relation independently revives the banished target and fails; --send-gated-level wrongly suppresses the Macro Level change and fails. Separate control traces preserve these expected failures.

Goleaf fixtures: complete production effects, neutral Deck/revival cards, actual setup/excavation/Summon and chained removal Spells, full official Macro Lua seeded face-up (its activation is not tested). No core/helper adapter is used. The original chained-removal fixture could respond to the setup Spell too early; it was corrected to require Goleaf already in GY, so removal actually responds after revival targeting. No production behavior was changed to fit the fixture. Open: hard revival count sharing/reset, soft ignition count reset through moves/turns, Level reset, source leaving before resolution, target leaving then returning, opponent-turn triggers, Necrovalley and native Omega. Current filesystem total 49/91 authored, 42 absent. Next missing script: Sylvan Flamioak (238276574).

Sylvan Flamushroomo (238276572), 2026-10-01: authored complete production Lua and two candidate prompts; syntax check and candidate row stats pass. Fresh missing-script count is 48/91 authored, 43 absent. Existing pinned official Sylvan c99641328 supplies the excavation pattern: ConfirmDecktop, Plant sending with EFFECT+REVEAL, sorting the other cards and moving each to Deck bottom. Flamushroomo explicitly requires Plant monsters, reveals selected non-public Sylvan hand cards during resolution (not as a cost), has both Normal/Special Summon triggers without imposing an unwritten count limit, and gives only its excavation recruitment a shared hard once-per-turn count.

`fresh_sylvan_flamushroomo.cjs` passes fifteen full-script public-core cases: actual Normal/Special Summon; one/three revealed cards; mixed Plant/non-Plant/Spell and all-Plant excavation; exact Deck-bottom sequences from two different selected permutations; decline; no Sylvan hand card; recruitment from hand, GY and self; ordinary effect-send rejection; REVEAL-tagged cost-send rejection; REVEAL/EFFECT-tagged hand-origin rejection; full-zone rejection; two copies actually placed on Deck top, revealed together and sent together, yielding exactly one recruitment. --no-count makes the two-copy case recruit twice and fail; --no-effect-reason makes the cost-send negative fail. Fixtures provide neutral hand/deck/recruitment cards and a real Spell that prepares top order, Summons or excavates/sends; negative cost/hand scenarios deliberately attach REVEAL with the wrong reason/source rather than pretending those are valid excavation. No production effects are omitted.

Sorting adapter qualification: local @n1xx1/ocgcore-wasm serializes SORT_CARD as length followed by permutation. Primary public core SortCard validates permutation bytes starting at byte zero: https://raw.githubusercontent.com/edo9300/ygopro-core/master/playerop.cpp (Processors::SortCard). The test-only iterable's length emits the first chosen index and its iterator emits the remaining indices, supplying exactly the correct bytes through the wrapper. Both actual bottom orders are queried, not inferred from a prompt. --wrapper-sort retains the unadapted RETRY failures in separate traces. Production Lua/helpers/core effects are unchanged by this adapter; native Omega remains untested. Open: short Deck versus hand reveal count semantics, already-public hand cards, on-Summon interruptions, unrelated excavation sources/replacements, Necrovalley/Macro and count reset across turns. Next absent script: Sylvan Goleaf (238276573).

Prayer Of Nephthys (238276252), 2026-10-01: authored production Quick-Play Lua, syntax checked, two candidate prompts staged. Fresh filesystem count: 47 of initial 91 missing scripts authored, 44 absent. `fresh_nephthys_prayer.cjs` passes eighteen full-script public-core cases: destroy a Level 8 victim on field or in hand; Summon two Level 2 Nephthys from hand/GY or both from hand; actual mandatory Summon trigger damage is negated, and an actually activated ignition damage effect is also negated; both Summoned monsters are actually destroyed at that turn's End Phase with DESTROY/EFFECT reasons. Illegal full-field, hand victim with only one free zone, wrong-Level victim and only one Level 2 candidate reject activation. Actual official Blue-Eyes Spirit Dragon Lua seeded face-up blocks simultaneous recruitment (its own Summon is not tested). A real destruction-protection effect on the victim prevents the Summons. A real chained Quick-Play fixture fills both remaining zones before Prayer resolves: Prayer still destroys the victim but cannot Summon two into the one remaining space.

Prayer GY branch: actual Ritual destruction allows banish cost/search from Deck, GY or face-up banishment, including explicit Phoenix Wing Wind Blast code 63356631 without Nephthys set membership. Face-down banishment, non-Ritual destruction and optional decline are checked. Simultaneous destruction of Prayer and the Ritual exposed an erroneous GY trigger; production now excludes its handler from that destruction event Group, implementing 'while this card is in your GY'. Chained zone-fill also exposed that activation-only zone legality had been reused during resolution, incorrectly preventing even the first destruction; production separates deslegal (activation) from desfilter (resolution). Independent --no-negation, --no-end-destruction and --same-event controls fail their respective positive/negative cases and preserve separate traces. All production effects stay loaded. Fixtures explicitly supply neutral victims/search targets and actual Level 2 Summon-trigger/ignition, destruction-protection, destruction and zone-fill effects; no engine events are mocked and no core/helper translation is used. Open: hard once-per-turn sharing/reset, opponent-turn Quick-Play timing, End Phase activation, leaving/re-entering field or control changes before delayed destruction, Necrovalley, redirection/replacements, search negation, and native Omega. Next absent script in baseline order: Sylvan Flamushroomo (238276572).

Incarnation Standby follow-up (2026-10-01): eight actual multi-turn cases pass in `fresh_nephthys_incarnation_standby.cjs`: shuffle two targets at turn-three own Standby; send-only rejection; optional decline does not carry into turn-five own Standby; only Incarnation in GY cannot satisfy target-two; Incarnation plus another Nephthys can be shuffled; destruction during the first own Standby does not permit the effect until the next own Standby; a real Quick-Play Spell chained to the shuffle banishes one target, so only the remaining target returns; the same physical target leaving then returning to GY is also excluded. Production now uses Duel.GetChainInfo(0,CHAININFO_TARGET_CARDS):Filter(Card.IsRelateToEffect,nil,e), replacing the missing Duel.GetTargetCards helper. The whole Incarnation script remains loaded, with neutral GY monsters and actual destroy/send/chain-removal fixture Spells. No events or target relations are fabricated.

Engine qualification: pinned Omega defines CHAININFO_TARGET_CARDS=0x40, whereas the public core expects enum 8. The harness translates only that constant in memory; the production script and pinned helper files keep their native symbolic API. Primary current constants: https://raw.githubusercontent.com/ProjectIgnis/CardScripts/master/constant.lua (enum 8). Current primary utility's GetTargetCards wrapper uses GetChainInfo plus the same relation filter: https://raw.githubusercontent.com/ProjectIgnis/CardScripts/master/utility.lua. --legacy-chain-flag preserves the unadapted API errors separately; these adapted passes are not native Omega certification. --no-relation independently fails both chained target-loss cases, and --short-own-standby fails the destruction-during-own-Standby case. Total Incarnation focused cases is sixteen. Still open: non-Ritual revival, player/control variations, shared counts and resets, interruptions/negation/destruction prevention of copying, and native Omega. New-script count stays 46 authored/45 absent.

Incarnation of Nephthys (238276250), 2026-10-01: authored full production Lua and three candidate prompts; syntax check and candidate staging pass. Fresh filesystem count is 46/91 authored, 45 absent. `fresh_nephthys_incarnation_copy.cjs` passes eight actual public-core cases with complete Incarnation/Sacred Feather/official Rebirth scripts: Sacred Feather cost from Deck or hand, one/two field Tributes, actual EVENT_RELEASE trigger instead of destruction, self-only-material rejection, Deck Tribute after self-destruction clears the field, and official Rebirth with one/two Tributes. Positive queries verify proper Ritual completion status, sent Spell COST reason, actual materials carrying RELEASE reason, Incarnation banishment as its GY trigger cost, and destruction of one/two opposing monsters matching actual material count. Neutral Ritual/material monsters and a real trigger Spell are explicit fixtures; no core events or production effect operations are mocked.

Runtime caught the nonexistent EFFECT_CANNOT_BE_RITUAL_MATERIAL constant in the first draft. Production temporarily registers EFFECT_UNRELEASABLE_NONSUM while checking the sent Spell's activation target, excluding the handler that must first destroy itself; the restriction is reset after each check. The independent `--no-material-block` control skips registration and fails the self-only case because an illegal copy is offered. Its trace is separate from passing production traces. Open: next-own-Standby shuffle-two effect, declining/losing targets, destruction during own Standby, non-Ritual revival of a previously Ritual Summoned monster, both players' Ritual triggers, shared first-effect count limits, negation/self-destruction prevention and replacement chains, damage-step timing, and native Omega. Sacred Feather's GY Trap-placement branch remains open. This supersedes older entries saying Incarnation remains absent.

Incarnation prerequisite audit (2026-10-01): extended `fresh_nephthys_sacred_feather.cjs` to eight passing cases, adding a real Level 4 Ritual Summon using two Level 2 field monsters. The Ritual fixture's actual mandatory Summon trigger verifies IsSummonType(RITUAL), GetMaterial count (one or two), and RELEASE reason on every recorded material, then deals 377 damage once as proof the probe resolved. Each material's actual EVENT_RELEASE trigger independently deals 177 damage, giving two such triggers in the two-material case. Deck and Macro-redirection positives also pass this material-identity/reason probe. This establishes an actual material-count source for Incarnation's destruction effect rather than guessing from Levels or field size. Multiple mandatory triggers exposed a fixture response error; SELECT_CHAIN now selects a mandatory effect instead of declining, and subgroup completion correctly responds with a null index. Production Lua was unchanged in this follow-up. Incarnation is still absent: activation-effect copying and exclusion of its self-destroyed handler from prospective materials require implementation and duel evidence. Count remains 45 authored/46 absent; full audit/native Omega remains open.

Sacred Feather Deck-Tribute follow-up supersedes the two failures below: the Deck branch now uses SendtoGrave with EFFECT+RELEASE+MATERIAL+RITUAL reasons, preserving the printed Tribute rather than replacing it with an ordinary send. Current primary core source explicitly raises individual/group EVENT_RELEASE when the move has REASON_RELEASE (`https://raw.githubusercontent.com/edo9300/ygopro-core/master/operations.cpp`, send-to step 10). `fresh_nephthys_sacred_feather.cjs` now passes seven complete-production cases: actual field-material Ritual Summon; Deck Tribute for Level 2 and Level 8 Rituals; unrelated/insufficient-Level rejection; occupied own Monster Zone blocking the alternate Deck branch; and actual official Macro Cosmos redirecting the Deck-Tributed material to banishment while the Ritual still succeeds. The material fixture's actual mandatory EVENT_RELEASE effect deals 177 damage exactly once in every positive case. Queries assert both RELEASE/RITUAL reasons and the Ritual monster's proper-procedure status bit. Macro's full official Lua is loaded, seeded face-up; its own activation is not tested. No engine events or release reasons are mocked.

Independent in-memory controls: `--old-deck-release` restores the old Release call and fails the Deck positives; `--ordinary-send` removes only RELEASE reason and fails the reason assertion; `--old-relation` retains the earlier field-Summon regression. `--deck-api` tries ReleaseRitualMaterial(mat,true) in memory: in the bundled public core it moves the Deck material and Summons, but loses RELEASE reason/Tribute semantics, so it remains a diagnostic FAIL and was not adopted. This public engine differs from current primary core source, which has a release_deck boolean (`https://raw.githubusercontent.com/edo9300/ygopro-core/master/libduel.cpp` and `field.cpp`). The pinned Omega reference scripts contained no examples using that boolean; native support is unproven. Production uses established SendtoGrave and explicit reasons, with real EVENT_RELEASE evidence. Open: native Omega, release prohibition/immunity, replacement chains, full field normal-material selection, changing Level/name, and the GY Trap-placement branch. New-script count remains 45 authored/46 absent; this turn fixes an existing script needed by Incarnation's Ritual-copy branch.

Apostle (238276249) continuation: authored production Lua and four staged prompts. `fresh_nephthys_apostle_hand.cjs` passes seven complete-script duels: destruction/hand Summon and conditional Nephthys Spell search from Deck or GY, unrelated victim without search, optional decline, full-zone/no-other-card rejection, and Normal Summon opponent-monster destruction. The victim fixture has an actual GY trigger (777 damage) and GY ignition (100 damage); another same-passcode copy is initialized in GY. The destroyed physical card's trigger is never offered, only the other GY copy's ignition is offered, and that other copy actually deals 100 damage. In-memory `--no-lock` controls fail because the destroyed card's trigger reappears; `--name-lock` controls fail because they wrongly block the other copy. These negative-control traces are separate files, not production failures. `--reset` passes one multi-turn case: on the next own turn the previously destroyed physical copy's ignition becomes available and actually deals another 100 damage, demonstrating expiry of the restriction. No production effect code is omitted/mocked.

`fresh_nephthys_apostle_standby.cjs` passes four actual multi-turn cases: effect destruction enables revival only at the next own Standby; sending without destruction does not; declining revival does not preserve the chance into a later own Standby; filling all five main zones after destruction blocks revival. The complete Apostle script is loaded; a real fixture Spell destroys/sends it and the full-zone setup actually Summons a filler into the space. Total Apostle focused cases: twelve. Open: destruction prevention/redirection, negation, same physical victim through further moves/control changes, destruction during own Standby, opponent-turn hand timing, monster trigger shared counts/chains, and native Omega. Current new-script count: 45/91 authored; 46 absent.

Existing Sacred Feather (216532402) fresh Ritual audit: `fresh_nephthys_sacred_feather.cjs` exposed an invalid IsRelateToEffect check on the Ritual monster, which is selected during resolution rather than targeted. Production now checks that it remains in hand. The field-material positive case actually Tributes the material with RELEASE and RITUAL reasons and Summons the Ritual monster; unrelated material and insufficient total Level reject activation. In-memory `--old-relation` independently makes the field-material positive fail again. Two Deck-Tribute cases still FAIL: the Level 2 and Level 8 Ritual candidates are selected, as is their Deck material, but Duel.Release on that Deck Group moves no material and returns zero, ending the operation. This is an unresolved public-engine/runtime compatibility or implementation issue, not proof of native Omega support or a reason to replace the printed Tribute with an ordinary send-to-GY. Keep both failures visible. Full Sacred Feather certification, Deck-Tribute semantics/events, proper-procedure recovery, and its GY Trap-placement branch remain open; Incarnation's Ritual-copy implementation must account for these existing branches.

Nephthys continuation: Brazier (238276251) and Scion (238276248) are authored, syntax-valid, and staged with two/four prompts. Current filesystem total: 44 of the initial 91 missing scripts authored; 47 absent. These additions are not full-card verification.

`fresh_nephthys_brazier_activation.cjs` passes eight complete-production public-core duels: Deck/GY two-card searches; optional decline; Level 3 low/Level 7 high rejection; no-other-card and absent-high rejection; and destruction of a low-Level Nephthys from hand followed by recovery of that same card from GY plus the high-Level Deck monster. The latter verifies an activation must consider a destruction victim becoming a valid GY search candidate, and its actual destruction also triggers Brazier to target/destroy an opponent monster. A second Brazier in hand cannot activate that turn after the first activation, including when its optional search is declined. Companion monsters have neutral scripts. `fresh_nephthys_scion_hand.cjs` passes six complete-script cases, observing actual reveal confirmation, destruction of a field Nephthys, and actual Scion Summon. A full field permits Summon by destroying the Nephthys occupying a main zone. Ritual Level 2 and ordinary Level 8 searches succeed; Level 9 non-Ritual and unrelated Deck monsters are excluded; Normal Summon search succeeds too. A second Scion's Quick Effect is unavailable after first use. This fixture does not verify every destruction choice or shared search counts.

`fresh_nephthys_scion_standby.cjs` passes seven actual multi-turn duels. A real fixture Spell destroys Scion (or only sends it as a negative case); recovery occurs at the next own Standby, never the opponent's, and does not persist into a later own Standby after decline. Recovering Scion itself is legal and does not Special Summon it from hand. Phoenix variants load the actual official c61441708.lua and destroy Sacred Phoenix alongside Scion. Resolving Phoenix's actual Standby activation first enables Scion's later optional revival; accepting Summons Scion and declining leaves it in GY. Resolving Scion's recovery before Phoenix's activation correctly grants no revival, even though Phoenix activates later that phase. No Phoenix flags are mocked. Fixture ordering was corrected to choose Phoenix first for positive cases; the opposite order is an explicit negative. Open: destruction during own Standby, opponent destruction/activation, leave/re-entry, disabled/different-name Phoenix effects, negation/chain changes, full zones at revival, and native Omega. Full 711-card audit remains unfinished.

Mega Staar (238273772) continuation: authored production script and three staged prompts. Synchro materials are exactly one WIND Tuner plus one or more non-Tuner WIND Pendulum Synchros. Pendulum Tribute Summon, field-wide Counter reductions (300 in PZone/200 in MZone), opponent targeting/destruction protection, soft once-per-turn Counter banishment with own face-up Field Zone Amplifire, and optional opponent-caused leave-field placement are implemented. `fresh_symphonic_megastaar_pendulum.cjs` passes six cases for valid Tribute/Summon, full-zone space freed by Tribute, no Tribute, wrong Attribute, wrong Race, and non-Synchro rejection. This fixture uses a real Spell to place the hybrid in PZone and explicitly calls CompleteProcedure; it does not prove proper Synchro Summon or legal acquisition of that PZone state. Production effects are unmodified.

`fresh_symphonic_megastaar_counters.cjs` passes seven cases using actual Counters on both fields and the actual official Amplifire script. With three Counters the opponent loses 600 ATK/DEF in MZone or 900 in PZone. Removing two Counters banishes two field/GY cards; removing three permits banishing only one. Actual REMOVE_COUNTER messages and resulting banishment are asserted; no BECOME_TARGET occurs. The soft count prevents reuse despite legal cards/Counters remaining. Missing, opponent-controlled, face-down Amplifire, and zero Counters block the Quick Effect. Fixture holders permit Counters and a neutral Spell adds them; the card is seeded on field without proving Synchro Summon. Later restrictions, changes after cost, two copies, and reset remain open.

`fresh_symphonic_megastaar_leave.cjs` passes seven of eight cases with complete production Lua: an opponent chained Quick-Play actually sends the hybrid toward GY (redirecting to face-up Extra) or banishes it, then its trigger places it in PZone. Own send, declined placement, and full PZones do not place it. Opponent destruction fails and own destruction succeeds, proving actual effect-destruction protection. A fixture IsCanBeEffectTarget probe confirms opponent-target rejection/own-target acceptance where the monster remains present; no real targeted effect is activated. The opponent return-to-hand case returns the hybrid to face-down Extra and then FAILS because the public core lacks Duel.CheckSynchroMaterial when evaluating its registered procedure. Preserve this as an engine-blocked case, not a pass; neither procedure registration nor this move's placement behavior is certified. Native Omega, real material selection/Summon, battle destruction, target/handler chains, resets, and broader immunity remain open. Current new scripts: 42/91 authored, 49 absent; authored is not verified.

Disccs genuine face-up Extra recruitment: `fresh_symphonic_disccs_recruit_extra.cjs` now passes. A real setup Spell sends a neutral Pendulum from MZone toward GY, the engine redirects it to face-up Extra, and Disccs returns itself as cost and actually Summons it. Selection asserts LOCATION_EXTRA, avoiding the earlier direct-initialization fixture pitfall. This extends Disccs to twelve focused cases; no actual Pendulum/Synchro Summon or native Omega certification is implied.

DDJ Pendulum follow-up: four `fresh_symphonic_ddj_pendulum.cjs` cases pass actual hand placement followed by the non-targeting flip ignition, rejection of unrelated/face-up/opponent-controlled monsters, and repeat-use rejection. Five `fresh_symphonic_ddj_response.cjs` cases exposed and fixed two production defects: EVENT_CHAINING needs QUICK_O registration to offer the response, and a Pendulum-zone card is treated as a Spell, so a TYPE_MONSTER filter incorrectly rejected the partner. The corrected full script actually returns DDJ to hand and Summons the other Pendulum after a neutral Symphonic monster's ignition. Unrelated monster activation, absent/unrelated partner, and full zones reject the response. Pendulum Spell activations during fixture setup must not offer the response. In-memory `--old-type` and `--old-filter` controls independently fail the positive scenario, while production passes all 20 DDJ cases across four harnesses. Controls write separate traces. Open: opponent's monster effect, Damage Step, chained return/partner failure, negation, resets, search shared counts, and optional immediate Synchro. Pinned Omega `procedure.lua` SynCondition/SynTarget passes a compulsory non-Tuner through Check/SelectSynchroMaterial rather than forcing it to be a Tuner; this is source evidence, not runtime proof of DDJ's Synchro branch.

Disccs (238273770) is newly authored, syntax-valid, and staged with four prompts. Its search includes official Generalprobe (82735249), Symph Amplifire (75304793), and Miccs (5399521), plus pinned custom Mega Staar (238273772), derived from local official/pinned text mentioning Symphonic Counters; Disccs itself is excluded. The script implements shared event-clone counts, whole-turn face-down Extra Deck restrictions, per-player/per-chain monster-effect gating, return costs/recruitment, and face-up Extra Deck arrival after Synchro material with three field Counters as cost. Eleven complete-script public-core duels pass across `fresh_symphonic_disccs_search.cjs` (four), `fresh_symphonic_disccs_pendulum.cjs` (three), and `fresh_symphonic_disccs_recruit.cjs` (four). These prove Normal Summon archetype/Generalprobe search, own-name/no-candidate exclusion, actual Pendulum-zone return cost and Summon including all five main zones occupied, no-other-card cost rejection, hand/GY recruitment, full-zone recruitment using the returned monster's space, and self-recruitment after return. Generalprobe's effect is stubbed, but its official passcode and Spell type are actual inputs. The search fixture has no face-down Extra Deck Summon; restriction correctness is unverified. Open: Special Summon search, prior/later locks, genuine face-up Extra recruitment, Counter/material trigger, same-chain gates, chains/immunity, resets/count sharing, and native Omega. Current filesystem total: 41 of 91 scripts authored; 50 absent. Authored is not verified.

DDJ continuation (2026-09-30): reviewed production c238273769.lua against the pinned baseline text; added all six carrier-safe prompt strings and rebuilt the candidate database without replacing the distributed database. `fresh_symphonic_ddj_hand.cjs` passes six complete-script public-core duels: legal Quick hand Summon and Deck search, no-archetype and full-zone rejection, Normal Summon search, own-name search, and Summon without a Deck search candidate. `fresh_symphonic_ddj_flip.cjs` passes five complete-script public-core duels. A fixture Spell actually flips DDJ face-up; the mandatory trigger activates and Summons a Level 4 Symphonic face-down Defense, while Level 5, own-name, unrelated, and full-zone cases Summon nothing. Lua syntax and three September banlist tests pass. Open: both Pendulum effects, chains/negation, restriction expiry, search shared counts, optional immediate Synchro including non-Tuner treatment, and native Omega.
