Mettronomo arriving-restriction chains: eleven Deck cases now pass. A real chained Quick-Play fixture Summons the actual scripted Blue-Eyes Spirit Dragon before Mettronomo resolves. The former Level 2+3 group becomes unavailable and nothing is Summoned, while the Tribute remains paid in GY. A one-card Level 5 group still resolves. The fixture explicitly bypasses Spirit Dragon's proper-Summon condition to establish its field restriction; it does not test its own legal Summon procedure. Response activation, Spirit Dragon on field, and Mettronomo's paid cost are asserted. Initial setup/placement failures were fixed in the fixture (face-down Quick-Play, opponent-zone selection), not production code.

Mettronomo Deck restriction/current-Level follow-up: nine cases now pass. The actual Omega Blue-Eyes Spirit Dragon script on the opponent's field prevents the two-card Level 2+3 group while allowing one Level 5 Deck monster. A real continuous +1 Level effect on Mettronomo changes its field Level from 5 to 6: Levels 2+4 are Summoned and Levels 2+3 are rejected, proving cost eligibility/resolution use current Level rather than printed metadata. These tests load both production Mettronomo and the official Spirit Dragon script; no one-at-a-time marker is mocked. Chained changes after cost, name-changing effects, resets, and native Omega remain open.

Mettronomo hand procedure evidence: `fresh_symphonic_mettronomo_hand.cjs` passes actual Special Summon with a face-up controlled Amplifire, and rejects facedown, opponent-controlled, absent Amplifire, and full main Monster Zones. Two Mettronomo copies are seeded in hand; after the first successful procedure the second is unavailable despite remaining zones and unchanged Amplifire, verifying the shared once-per-turn procedure limit. Amplifire's own effect script is stubbed; its passcode/type/position/control are real fixture inputs. Turn reset, removal/re-entry, and native Omega remain open.

Mettronomo GY adjustment evidence: `fresh_symphonic_speeaker_hand.cjs --mettronomo-level` and the additional `--decrease` option pass six runs covering Level 3 to 4 or 2, no archetype target rejection, and a Level 1 target that receives only +1 even with all main Monster Zones occupied. The test asserts Mettronomo actually banished as cost, actual resulting target Level, and no decrease choice at Level 1. Both directions load the complete production Lua. Hand Summon, chained target loss, immunity, lasting Level/reset behavior, and shared count limits remain open.

Mettronomo exact-Level runtime evidence: `fresh_symphonic_mettronomo_deck.cjs` loads the complete production script and passes five cases: Level 5 Tribute summons distinct Levels 2+3; two same-name Level 2 copies are rejected despite matching a Level 4 Tribute; distinct Levels 2+2 reject a Level 5 Tribute; with four other monsters, a two-monster group is unavailable; one Level 5 Deck monster is correctly Summoned into the zone freed by Tributing Mettronomo. Positive cases verify actual Tribute into GY and actual Deck monsters on the field. Native Omega, one-at-a-time restriction, dynamic Level changes, chain interruption, name-changing effects, and other two effects remain open.

Symphonic Warrior Mettronomo (238273771) is authored and syntax-valid, with staged prompts. Deck Summon cost eligibility checks exact total Level, distinct card names, and zones freed by the selected Tribute; resolution recomputes available legal subsets and includes the one-at-a-time Summon restriction. Its other effects implement face-up controlled Amplifire hand Summon and GY banishment/targeted +/-1 Level choice. Runtime coverage remains open for every effect, especially subgroup selection, duplicate names, dynamic Level snapshot, full zones, shared procedure counts, and resets. Authored additions: 39 of 91; 52 remain absent.

Speeaker Pendulum boundary follow-up: five Pendulum cases pass. A second actual Speeaker copy as the only Deck candidate correctly prevents the replacement effect. A fixture occupying the other Pendulum-capable Spell/Trap Zone (sequence 4) does not prevent destruction/replacement into the freed sequence 0 zone, and its occupant is preserved. This second fixture verifies occupied-zone handling, not effects of a second Pendulum. Destruction replacement, target/handler chain movement, duplicate count limits, and turn reset remain open.

Speeaker Pendulum evidence: `fresh_symphonic_speeaker_hand.cjs --pendulum` passes real hand-to-Pendulum placement, effect destruction into face-up Extra Deck, and Deck replacement into the freed Pendulum Zone. It rejects an unrelated Pendulum and a Symphonic non-Pendulum as sole replacement candidates. In this mode the harness's `full` fixture flag means the candidate lacks Pendulum type, not occupied zones. The first positive check incorrectly required FACEUP_ATTACK; engine movement uses FACEUP_DEFENSE for the destroyed card in Extra Deck. The assertion now accepts either face-up position, and all three cases pass. Same-name exclusion, destruction replacement, occupied second Pendulum Zone, chain interactions, and count-limit reset remain open. Hand-effect regressions also pass.

Speeaker later restriction evidence: `fresh_underroot_terror_search.cjs --speeaker --later=<type>` passes fusion, pendulum-fusion, wind-synchro, and faceup-pendulum scenarios after both Normal/Special Summon recruitment. The helper checks the core's IsCanBeSpecialSummoned result: face-down non-WIND Fusion and Pendulum Fusion are disallowed; WIND Synchro and a genuinely face-up Extra Deck Pendulum are allowed and actually Summoned. The Pendulum is moved field-to-Extra by the engine before the probe. Recruitment completion is asserted before testing the lock. Eight cases pass. End Phase reset, activation negation, unsuccessful recruitment, and native Omega remain open.

Speeaker fixture correction and stronger evidence: direct initialization of a normal Pendulum in Extra Deck silently placed it in Deck in this public core. New assertions on selected recruitment-card location exposed the earlier face-up Extra Deck claims as insufficient; those earlier results are superseded, not retained as proof. The Extra Deck recruitment fixture now moves a seeded field Pendulum toward GY through an actual Spell effect, asserts that the engine redirects it to face-up Extra Deck, and asserts EXTRA as its selected recruitment source. Both Normal/Special Summon cases now pass with those assertions. Prior face-up Pendulum activity uses the same field-to-Extra movement before an asserted actual Extra Deck Special Summon; both subsequent recruitment events pass. Deck/hand fixtures also now check the selected source location. These corrections concern test setup, not production Speeaker Lua.

Speeaker prior-activity restriction evidence: recruitment is correctly unavailable after an earlier effect Summon from face-down Extra Deck of a non-WIND Fusion, including a Pendulum Fusion. An earlier WIND Synchro effect Summon permits recruitment. Each case passes subsequent Normal and Special Summon events (six cases). The prior Summon fixture explicitly asserts success, then clears the summoned monster to avoid occupied Extra Monster Zone interference. The face-up Pendulum prior-activity fixture currently fails because its card is not found by the Extra Deck selection; this is fixture failure, not a confirmed card defect or pass. Later lock enforcement, face-up prior-activity eligibility, and native Omega remain open. Tests invoke `fresh_underroot_terror_search.cjs --speeaker --prior=fusion|wind-synchro|pendulum-fusion|faceup-pendulum` with a single selected value per run.

Speeaker recruitment runtime evidence: `fresh_underroot_terror_search.cjs --speeaker` passes Deck recruitment after both Normal and effect Special Summon. `--recruit-hand` and `--recruit-extra` each pass those two summon events as well, covering six source/event combinations. The Extra Deck fixture is a face-up Pendulum with actual Pendulum metadata; it is Summoned from Extra Deck using the production zone check. The complete production Speeaker script is loaded. The underlying Terror Skull search regressions remain green. These cases do not yet demonstrate prior-turn-activity eligibility, later Extra Deck lock enforcement, facedown Extra Deck rejection, optional decline, chained target loss, or shared event count limits.

Speeaker hand effect evidence: `fresh_symphonic_speeaker_hand.cjs` loads the complete production Lua and passes increasing a controlled Symphonic Warrior from Level 3 to 4 followed by Speeaker's Special Summon, rejects a non-Symphonic target, and rejects activation with all five main Monster Zones occupied. The Deck has no recruitment target, isolating the hand effect without omitting any registered effects. Pendulum replacement, summon-trigger recruitment, whole-turn Extra Deck restrictions, target-loss chains, immunity, and count-limit reset remain open.

Symphonic Warrior Speeaker (238273768) is authored, syntax-valid, and staged with prompts. The script implements Pendulum self-destruction/Deck replacement excluding its name, targeted Level increase followed by hand Summon, Normal/Special Summon recruitment, and the whole-turn restriction on Summons from face-down Extra Deck except WIND Synchros. The activity filter distinguishes prior Extra Deck position rather than treating all Pendulums as face-up Extra Deck cards; face-down Pendulum Fusion/Synchro cards must not gain a blanket exemption. All effects remain runtime-UNTESTED, especially activity-counter timing, prior/later Extra Deck restrictions, face-up Extra Deck zones, chained target loss, and Pendulum replacement. Authored additions: 38 of 91; 53 remain absent.

Afterroot material-Level API evidence: `fresh_underroot_afterroot_levels.cjs` passes rroot and unrelated summon-card probes using the complete production Lua. Printed Level remains 4. GetSynchroLevel/GetRitualLevel report packed choices 4 and 1 only for rroot; unrelated cards report 4 only. IsXyzLevel accepts 4 for both and 1 only for rroot, confirming the third-argument Xyz callback through the core. The initial GetXyzLevel probe failed because that method is absent; Omega's actual procedure uses IsXyzLevel, which is present and now used by the test. These are real engine material-Level queries, not completed Summons or material-selection/count-limit proof. Native proper Synchro/Xyz procedures, optional-use accounting in the source's broad once-per-turn clause, and generic Level-dependent Fusion procedures remain unresolved.

Afterroot type/revival eligibility follow-up: thirteen swap cases pass, now including Fusion, Synchro, Xyz, and Link target pairs, a mixed Synchro-field/Xyz-GY pair, rejection of an ordinary GY monster, and rejection of an Extra Deck GY monster with EnableReviveLimit that was not properly Summoned. The mixed pair is intentionally legal: the source says one "such monster," not the same Extra Deck Type. Positive revival cases still use unrestricted fixture monsters; the improper-Summon case supplies negative evidence only. No production change was needed for these cases.

Afterroot chain/zone follow-up: the swap harness now passes two actual chained Quick-Play Spell responses. Removing the field target to GY before resolution stops banishment and revival; returning the GY target to Extra Deck still allows the first target's banishment but offers no revival. Both verify the response really activated. A full opponent main Monster Zone scenario also passes: target eligibility accounts for the zone freed by banishing the first target, then revives the second into that space. Seven swap cases now pass; native Omega, other Extra Deck Types, banishment replacement, immune targets, reset duration, and Level treatment remain open.

Afterroot paired swap runtime evidence: `fresh_underroot_afterroot_swap.cjs` passes accepted and declined opponent revival, absent GY target rejection, and non-Extra-Deck field monster rejection. Acceptance verifies the first target in banishment, the second on the opponent's field, and removal of its scripted +700 ATK effect (1000 to 300) as evidence of effect negation. The yes/no prompt must belong to opponent player 1. Declining still banishes the first target and leaves the second in GY. The swap is not offered again that turn after either successful activation. The full production Afterroot Lua is loaded; fixture Extra Deck monsters have no revive-limit procedure and start directly in field/GY, so proper-Summon restrictions and native Omega are not proven. Target-loss chains, full zones, other Extra Deck Types, and Level-1 material selection remain open.

Afterroot follow-up evidence: `fresh_underroot_overroot_set.cjs --afterroot` passes official Afterroot Trap selection without custom archetype tags, face-down Set, same-turn diagnostic Trap activation/resolution, and full Spell/Trap Zone rejection. As for Overroot, this fixture omits the other Quick Effect and native Extra Deck procedure registration in memory, and substitutes the Trap's activation script; it verifies only the Set branch. `fresh_underroot_terror_blossom_battle.cjs --afterroot` passes battle send/Defense Position revival, no-revival-target rejection, and summoning-player restriction rejection using the complete production Afterroot script with the boss initially on the field. The paired swap, optional Level treatment, proper Synchro Summon, and native Omega remain open.

Afterroot (238272440) now has an authored, syntax-valid script and staged prompts. It implements the exact two-material Synchro procedure, conditional original/Level-1 material choices for rroot Synchro/Xyz/Ritual Summons, official Afterroot Trap membership and same-turn activation, paired opponent Extra Deck monster banishment with opponent-controlled optional negated revival, and the battle send/revive effect. Xyz's callback uses its third argument (the summoned Xyz monster), not its second (the material). The existing rroot Fusion uses only archetype/type membership and no Level requirement; no rroot Ritual is in the pinned roster. Generic future Level-dependent Fusion procedures remain outside demonstrated coverage. All Afterroot effects remain runtime-UNTESTED, including native material selection, full zones, negation timing, target loss, and count limits. Current authored additions: 37 of 91; 54 scripts remain absent.

Overroot's Set effect now has isolated duel evidence: `fresh_underroot_overroot_set.cjs` passes selecting official Overroot Trap passcode 63086455 from Deck with no custom setcode, placing it face-down, activating it in the same turn, and resolving its diagnostic damage effect. With all five Spell/Trap Zones occupied, the Set trigger is not offered; `IsSSetable` already enforces that case, so no redundant zone patch was made. The fixture uses an effect Summon from Extra Deck and omits only attachment-effect and Xyz-procedure registration in memory because their public-core APIs are missing. It substitutes a diagnostic Trap script, not official Overroot's activation effect. This is isolated Set-effect evidence, not a full-card or native Omega pass.

Summoning-player regression now has sensitive runtime evidence: `fresh_underroot_terror_blossom_battle.cjs` passes ordinary revival, absent revival card, and a present GY monster whose EFFECT_SPSUMMON_CONDITION allows only opponent player 1 to perform the Summon. Player 0's battle effect correctly remains unavailable for that restricted monster. `--old-summoner-control` restores the old eligibility arguments only in memory and fails specifically because it incorrectly offers the battle effect for the restricted monster. The control does not edit production Lua. This validates the Fusion Blossom summoner fix; it does not resolve Overroot's attachment API gap.

Battle revival eligibility correction (238272438 and 238272439): checks previously treated the opponent as the player performing the Summon. They now pass the effect's activating player as summoner and explicitly pass the opponent as destination controller, matching the actual operation and Omega c85698115's opponent-field Summon pattern. Fusion Blossom's eligible/no-revival-target battle cases pass. Player-specific Summon restrictions need dedicated coverage; Overroot retains its public-core IsCanOverlay blocker.

Afterroot implementation planning: its source specifies exactly one Level 3 Tuner plus one non-Tuner. Omega provides conditional EFFECT_SYNCHRO_LEVEL and EFFECT_XYZ_LEVEL patterns, but no EFFECT_FUSION_LEVEL constant in the pinned helpers. Its broad "Level 1 for the Summon" text must not silently be reduced to only Synchro/Xyz support. No incomplete Afterroot script has been counted as authored.

Return-event sensitivity check: `fresh_underroot_terror_blossom_zones.cjs --event-check` adds a real single EVENT_TO_GRAVE damage trigger on the banished target. Returning it produces no damage. However, `--event-check --ordinary-send-control` deliberately strips REASON_RETURN only in the in-memory script and still produces no trigger; the control fails its expected-damage assertion. This public core therefore cannot distinguish the relevant event semantics on banishment-to-GY movement. The no-damage result is INCONCLUSIVE as proof of correct return-event suppression, not certification. Production scripts remain unchanged by the control. Omega c11110218 and constant.lua remain the authoritative source for the REASON_RETURN correction.

Follow-up official-Trap regressions: `fresh_underroot_nether_shuffle.cjs --official-traps` passes recycling Underroot and Overroot Traps without custom setcodes, and rejects activation with no controlled Extra Deck monster types. `fresh_underroot_terror_blossom_alt.cjs --official-trap` passes the official Underroot Trap search after alternate Summon, insufficient-material rejection, and repeat-Summon limit/reset cases. Both fixtures stub the Trap's own script; these results verify membership, not Trap activation. Ordinary Nether recycling also remains green.

Return-to-GY semantics corrected in Nether Skull's zone movement and Fusion Terror Blossom's zone swap: banished targets now use `REASON_EFFECT+REASON_RETURN`, matching Omega c11110218. Omega's constant.lua explicitly excludes REASON_RETURN from EVENT_TO_GRAVE. The Nether movement and Fusion zone-swap duels pass after this correction; dedicated cross-card event-trigger suppression remains untested.

## Scope and evidence standard

The independent baseline (`python scripts/fresh_ccg_baseline.py --remote`) uses pinned `origin/main` revision `b885aad510cc6cd685c00e43261881a5a6649fc6`, containing 711 website cards. The local merge union contains 636 cards and remains unresolved. The user explicitly requested that all website cards absent from Omega be added and coded. The initial remote comparison identified 91 missing scripts and database rows; 45 scripts are now authored in the working tree, leaving 46. Authored does not mean verified. The remaining 620 had both at the start of this audit. Existing working changes are preserved.

Every card starts UNTESTED. Syntax, registration, text matching, and isolated callback results do not confer effect correctness. Complete verification requires effect-text requirements, appropriate Omega reference scripts, legal and illegal activation cases, actual resolution outcomes, relevant chain interactions, restrictions and resets. Test infrastructure failures must not be counted as passes or attributed to card defects without investigation.

Terror Blossom from Overroot (238272439) is authored and syntax-valid, with staged prompts, but NOT runtime-verified. The parameterized battle harness (`fresh_underroot_terror_blossom_battle.cjs --overroot`) exposes a public-core API gap: `Card.IsCanOverlay` is unavailable during attachment eligibility evaluation. Both cases are recorded as FAIL, not passes; native Omega attachment, placement, battle, and Xyz procedure coverage remains open. Attachment resolution follows Omega c100259006 by sending the target's existing materials to GY before overlaying the card as a Group.

Official Trap membership correction: Terrors of the Underroot (11110218), Overroot (63086455), and Afterroot (85698115) do not carry the generated custom archetype tags. Explicit passcode membership was added to Terror Skull's other-card recovery, Nether Skull's rroot recycling, Fusion Terror Blossom's Underroot search, and Xyz Terror Blossom's Overroot Trap Set. `fresh_underroot_terror_return.cjs --official-trap` passes recovery from both GY and banishment using Trap metadata with no custom setcode; the Trap's own activation script is stubbed in this focused recovery fixture. The ordinary recovery regression also passes. The other corrected filters still require dedicated runtime cases.

The baseline records input hashes and hashes of the local Omega Lua reference tree. Its statuses are an immutable-in-purpose starting inventory, not current progress; scenario reports carry the tested source hashes. Regenerating the baseline does not import scenario results.

Terror Blossom from Underroot (238272438) is authored as an rroot Fusion with two rroot materials and a separate GY-banish Extra Deck Summon procedure. `fresh_underroot_terror_blossom_alt.cjs` passes that procedure, its ensuing Underroot Deck search, and rejection with only one material. A two-copy scenario frees the occupied Extra Monster Zone and retains two GY materials: the second alternate Summon is blocked that turn and succeeds next turn. A revival fixture first reproduced an illegal ordinary GY revival after the first proper Summon. Removing the Extra-Deck-only range from the mandatory Summon restriction fixes that violation of “Must be either…”. `fresh_underroot_terror_fusion.cjs --boss` also passes proper Fusion Summoning of this actual card with field/hand materials, confirming the restriction still permits Fusion. These material fixtures use neutral `GetMustMaterial`/`CheckMustMaterial` bindings absent from the public core; native Omega remains unverified.

Its opponent-zone tests pass moving GY and banished targets in opposite directions and rejecting activation without targets. An unavailable `Duel.GetTargetCards` call was fixed by retaining selected targets and checking relation at resolution. Its battle tests pass sending the battling opponent monster to GY and replacing it on their field in Defense Position with a different-name GY monster, plus rejecting activation without that mandatory revival candidate. Chained target loss and native Omega remain open. Current authored additions: 35 of 91; 56 scripts remain absent.

Blossom Skull from Underroot (238272435) is authored with staged prompts. `fresh_underroot_blossom_swap.cjs` passes an opponent-owned Fusion swap: the targeted monster returns to that owner's Extra Deck, the owner chooses a different same-Type/Level Fusion, and a test-only summon-trigger effect is suppressed for a non-rroot replacement but resolves for an rroot replacement. A wrong-Level replacement is unavailable, and the owner may decline a legal replacement. The parameterized `fresh_underroot_terror_fusion.cjs --blossom` passes its rroot Fusion Summon using field/hand materials and a two-Level decrease while Blossom remains on the field; a no-Extra-target case suppresses activation. `fresh_underroot_terror_return.cjs --blossom` passes self-revival after GY-to-banishment and banishment-to-GY moves. The Fusion test uses neutral bindings for public-core functions absent from Omega's procedure implementation, not native Omega certification. Synchro/Xyz branches, other swap Extra Deck Types, chains, turn resets, and native Omega remain unverified. Current authored additions: 34 of 91; 57 scripts remain absent.

Terror Skull from Underroot (238272434) now has an authored script and staged prompts. `fresh_underroot_terror_fusion.cjs` passes an actual Quick-Effect Fusion Summon using Terror Skull on the field and another rroot monster in hand, plus a separate legal Fusion using other materials that leaves Terror on the field at Level 1 after choosing a two-Level decrease. No Quick Effect is offered without an rroot Extra Deck target. The public core lacks Omega's `Duel.GetMustMaterial`/`Duel.CheckMustMaterial`; the test supplies neutral bindings only for these no-mandatory-material fixtures, so it is not native Omega certification. An attempted Rank 1 Xyz test reached the public runner's missing `Duel.CheckXyzMaterial` binding and was not counted. `fresh_underroot_terror_search.cjs` passes the Underroot monster search after Normal and Special Summon. `fresh_underroot_terror_return.cjs` passes adding Terror itself plus another Underroot card from the same destination after GY-to-banishment and banishment-to-GY moves. Synchro/Xyz branches, chained target loss, once-per-turn resets, and native Omega remain unverified. Current authored additions: 33 of 91; 58 scripts remain absent.

Nether Skull from Underroot (238272436) is authored with staged prompts and setcodes. `fresh_underroot_nether_move.cjs` passes two real duels: a monster sent to own GY is targeted and banished before Nether Summons from hand; a monster banished from own Deck is targeted and returned to GY before the same Summon. `fresh_underroot_nether_send.cjs` confirms its Special Summon trigger sends an Underroot monster from Deck to GY and subsequently banishes a chosen GY card, while no Underroot Deck target suppresses the trigger. `fresh_underroot_nether_shuffle.cjs` confirms banishing Nether from GY as cost and shuffling two chosen rroot cards from GY/banishment with one Fusion and one Synchro controlled, while no qualifying Extra Deck Type suppresses activation. Its separate first trigger is offered on its own GY-cost banishment, as printed; the shuffle test declines it. Level decrease choices, chained target loss, once-per-turn resets, and native Omega remain unverified. Current authored additions: 32 of 91; 59 scripts remain absent.

Over Skull from Underroot (238272437) is authored as the first of the seven absent Underroot-group cards. It uses the staged Underroot/rroot setcodes and four signed-safe effect prompts. `fresh_underroot_over_banish.cjs` passes real-engine cases for self-banish from hand and field into an Underroot Deck Summon, rejection with no eligible Underroot target, Level 3 to Level 1 after another Underroot is Normal Summoned, no Level change after a non-Underroot Summon, and self-Summon after returning from banishment to either GY or hand. The GY test exposed that `EVENT_TO_GRAVE` did not fire for that banishment-to-GY move; an `EVENT_MOVE` trigger filtered to that destination fixes it. The printed duration of the non-Tuner treatment is unspecified; the implementation currently persists while this field instance remains. Conditional non-Tuner use in an actual rroot Synchro Summon, once-per-turn/reset cases, and native Omega remain unverified. Current authored additions: 31 of 91; 60 scripts remain absent.

Terrarumian Trumpet Pitchers (284639724) now has an authored script and staged prompts. Public-core duels pass the Pendulum effect's own-monster requirement, destruction and Defense Position Summon, and optional face-up Extra Deck Summon; the targeting lock blocks only opponent targeting of own Terrarumian monsters. Its hand trigger succeeds after destruction of an own Terrarumian Pendulum and is absent for a non-Terrarumian Pendulum or non-Pendulum Terrarumian. Defense Position attack uses ATK. `fresh_terrarumian_trumpet_battle_chain.cjs` confirms an opponent Trap activation during the Battle Phase triggers destruction of an opponent monster and recovery of its 1300 original DEF, whereas activation outside Battle Phase does neither. The same test uses a test-only direct-attack effect on another Terrarumian Pendulum: actual 500 direct battle damage then triggers Trumpet to destroy a still-present opponent monster and recover its 1300 original DEF without any opponent chain activation. A fourth duel causes both triggers in one Battle Phase while retaining a second legal destruction target; only the first effect resolves, proving the shared once-per-turn limit in this scenario. The chain duel exposed a real invalid `GetOriginalDefense` call; replacing it with Omega-compatible `GetBaseDefense` fixes the resolution. Native Omega behavior and other timing edges remain unverified. Current authored additions: 30 of 91; 61 scripts remain absent.

Terrarumian Sundew (284639723) now has an authored script and staged prompts. `node --no-warnings scripts/fresh_terrarumian_sundew_pendulum.cjs` passes no-own-monster rejection, one- and two-card Pendulum-effect destruction, Defense Position Summon, and optional recovery of a genuinely face-up Pendulum from the Extra Deck. `node --no-warnings scripts/fresh_terrarumian_sundew_quick_end.cjs` verifies the opponent-response Quick Effect is offered only while Sundew is Pendulum Summoned in face-up Defense Position; destroying a Pendulum enables one End Phase shuffle, while destroying a non-Pendulum does not. A test-only Summon Spell supplies the Pendulum summon type; the actual Pendulum procedure is not covered. `node --no-warnings scripts/fresh_terrarumian_sundew_two_count.cjs` verifies two Pendulums destroyed by the Pendulum effect are both shuffled from the face-up Extra Deck at End Phase, establishing that the count covers both of Sundew's effects. Its Defense Position ATK-based battle calculation also passes. Full Monster Zones, response-chain edge cases, destruction replacement, and native Omega remain open. Current authored additions: 29 of 91; 62 scripts remain absent.

Terrarumian Ivy (284639722) now has an authored script and staged prompts. `node --no-warnings scripts/fresh_terrarumian_ivy_battle_lock.cjs` compares two real battles with the same opponent Trap: outside a Terrarumian battle the Trap is offered and adds 1000 effect damage; when a Terrarumian battles, the Trap is unavailable while ordinary 500 battle damage still occurs. `node --no-warnings scripts/fresh_terrarumian_staghorn_pendulum.cjs 284639722` passes absent, declined, and accepted optional Pendulum revival from the GY, plus no-own-monster rejection. `node --no-warnings scripts/fresh_terrarumian_ivy_destroyed.cjs` passes both destroyed-trigger choices, both-options selection, no-target rejection, actual targeting messages, and ATK normalization from 1500 to 1000 as evidence of effect negation on the chosen monster. The shared Defense Position test passes ATK-based battle calculation. Native Omega, negation lasting across turns, target-leaves-chain, and full-zone cases remain open. Current authored additions: 28 of 91; 63 scripts remain absent.

Terrarumian Staghorn Fern (284639721) now has an authored script and staged prompts. `node --no-warnings scripts/fresh_terrarumian_staghorn_chain.cjs` verifies an opponent response to a Terrarumian activation can be negated at resolution, preventing 1000 damage and destroying Staghorn; declining allows the damage and leaves it in the Pendulum Zone; a response to a non-Terrarumian activation does not offer negation. This focused test adapts only the chain-info flag constants: the public core uses 1/2 where the local Omega `constant.lua` uses 2/4, so native Omega confirmation is required. `node --no-warnings scripts/fresh_terrarumian_staghorn_pendulum.cjs` passes no-own-monster rejection and the optional second hand Pendulum Summon, including absent/declined/accepted cases. `node --no-warnings scripts/fresh_terrarumian_staghorn_destroyed.cjs` passes both destroyed-trigger choices, both-options choice handling, the Level 6 versus Level 7 target boundary, and actual `BECOME_TARGET` only for the opponent-monster branch. Its Defense Position ATK-based attack also passes. Chained target loss, full zones, once-per-turn repeat attempts, and native Omega remain open. Current authored additions: 27 of 91; 64 scripts remain absent.

Terrarumian Nerve Plant (284639720) now has an authored script and staged prompts. `node --no-warnings scripts/fresh_terrarumian_nerve_pendulum.cjs` passes no-own-monster prohibition, a +600 DEF boost from one own and one opposing Terrarumian monster, Special Summon after destroying another Terrarumian card with no GY Set target, and the optional GY Set with a target. `node --no-warnings scripts/fresh_terrarumian_nerve_destroy.cjs` passes Spell/Trap destruction after Normal Summon, Pendulum-effect Special Summon, and effect destruction, plus a no-target rejection. The shared Defense Position attack test verifies ATK-based battle calculation. Public-core evidence only; shared count-limit, chained target loss, non-Pendulum boost exclusion, and native Omega remain open. Current authored additions: 26 of 91; 65 scripts remain absent.

Terrarumian Sword Fern (284639719) now has an authored script and staged prompts. `node --no-warnings scripts/fresh_terrarumian_sword_fern_replacement.cjs` passes opponent-effect Field Zone destruction and banishment with Sword Fern destroyed instead, own-effect destruction without replacement, and declining the opponent-effect replacement. The first replacement duel exposed a missing `SetValue` callback on its field-wide `EFFECT_DESTROY_REPLACE`; adding it fixed actual resolution. `node --no-warnings scripts/fresh_terrarumian_sword_fern_set.cjs` passes the Spell/Trap Set trigger after Normal Summon, Pendulum-effect Special Summon, and effect destruction. Its Pendulum destroy/Summon/Deck placement and Defense Position attack also pass the parameterized Terrarumian tests. These are public-core tests, not native Omega certification; shared trigger count across different events, alternate replacement interactions, and broader chains remain open. Current authored additions: 25 of 91; 66 scripts remain absent.

Terrarumian Coin Plant (284639718) now has an authored Pendulum script with opponent ATK reduction, destruction/Summon/Deck placement, destroyed-by-effect banish choices, and Defense Position ATK-based attack. `node --no-warnings scripts/fresh_terrarumian_coin_pendulum.cjs` verifies that the Pendulum action is unavailable without an own Terrarumian monster, that one defending Terrarumian Pendulum reduces an opponent's 2000 ATK to 1700, and that the valid action destroys another Terrarumian card, Summons Coin Plant, and places a different Deck Pendulum. `node --no-warnings scripts/fresh_terrarumian_coin_banish.cjs` verifies a GY banish when the opponent has a hand card but the controller lacks two defending Terrarumians, and the alternative random face-up hand banish and End Phase return when two defenders are controlled. `node --no-warnings scripts/fresh_terrarumian_cushion_defense_attack.cjs 284639718` verifies an actual Defense Position attack uses ATK, not DEF. Multiple defending monsters' stacking, changed-field chain cases, banish redirection, and native Omega remain unverified. Current authored additions: 24 of 91; 67 scripts remain absent.

Terrarumian Cushion Moss (284639717) now has an authored Pendulum, destroyed-by-effect search, and Defense Position attack script with staged prompts. `node --no-warnings scripts/fresh_terrarumian_cushion_pendulum.cjs` passes three public-engine cases for its Pendulum effect: the no-monsters condition, the prohibited own-monster/no-opponent case, and the own-plus-opponent-monster condition. The successful cases actually destroy another Terrarumian, Summon Cushion Moss, and place a different Terrarumian Pendulum from Deck. The test activates Cushion Moss from hand first; direct insertion into the Pendulum Zone was not a valid public-engine setup. `node --no-warnings scripts/fresh_terrarumian_cushion_destroyed.cjs` passes both destroyed-by-effect cases: no search offer without another Terrarumian monster in Deck, and a successful search after a test Spell destroys Cushion Moss. `node --no-warnings scripts/fresh_terrarumian_cushion_defense_attack.cjs` revealed that `EFFECT_DEFENSE_ATTACK` value `1` uses DEF, contrary to this card's printed ATK calculation. Changing it to `0` makes the actual battle use ATK; the test deliberately separates the two stats (900 versus 3000) to detect regressions. Native Omega behavior and wider chain cases remain unverified. Current authored additions: 23 of 91; 68 scripts remain absent.

Candidate-database archetype audit: the website uses `Existing` and `New` as editorial labels for several of the 91 cards, not playable archetypes. The importer had assigned those labels shared synthetic setcodes (`0xbfde` and `0xaa02`), while many name-only series received no setcode. It now ignores those labels, applies Omega's official Symphonic Warrior (`0x1066`), Nephthys (`0x11f`), and Sylvan (`0x90`) codes, and assigns distinct collision-checked custom codes to rroot/Underroot/Overroot/Afterroot, Killamity, Kali Yuga, Terrarumian, Dark Swamp, and Hydra. `python scripts/stage_remote_omega.py` asserts group membership across all affected new cards and rejects placeholder setcodes across all 91. The public database remains untouched; this is in the staged candidate only. Focused existing Vylon, Machina, Bubble Colony, and banlist tests still pass after regeneration.

Terrarumian Spores (284636587) now has an authored Counter Trap script and staged prompts. `node --no-warnings scripts/fresh_terrarumian_spores_negate.cjs` passes five actual-chain cases: no activation without a controlled Terrarumian Pendulum monster; negate/destroy a Spell Card and optionally decline or accept destruction of your own Terrarumian; negate/destroy a monster effect activated on-field; and do not respond to an already-face-up Spell effect (the text specifies Spell/Trap *Card* activation). `node --no-warnings scripts/fresh_terrarumian_spores_set.cjs` passes two GY cases: no Set without a Terrarumian Tribute, and a successful Tribute/Set with leave-field banishment. The Tributed Pendulum correctly goes face-up to the Extra Deck. Opponent Trap activations, off-field monster effects, native Omega, and chain edge cases remain open. Current authored additions: 19 of 91; 72 scripts remain absent.

Terrarumian Misting (284636588) now has an authored Quick-Play script. `node --no-warnings scripts/fresh_terrarumian_misting_duels.cjs` passes five public-engine scenarios: no activation without a Terrarumian Pendulum, one target/one destruction/one negation, a second copy blocked in the same turn, two targets/two destructions/two negations, and one destruction being replaced so neither targeted card is negated. Opponent monsters carried test-only continuous +500 ATK effects so their negation was measured by actual attack values, not just script registration. The public core lacks Omega's target-group helper and rejects `CHAININFO_TARGET_CARDS`; the script instead retains the actual target group selected at activation, preserving relation checks and resolving correctly in both engines' supported API. Native Omega and target-leaves-chain edge cases remain open. Current authored additions: 20 of 91; 71 scripts remain absent.

Terrarumian Terrarium (284636586) now has an authored Field Spell script and staged choice prompts. `node --no-warnings scripts/fresh_terrarumian_terrarium_activation.cjs` passes no-Deck-target, declined search, add-from-Deck, and destroy-from-Deck cases. `node --no-warnings scripts/fresh_terrarumian_terrarium_battle.cjs` passes three actual-battle cases: the monster's displayed ATK stays unchanged outside damage calculation but its 900-point battle boost wins against a 1500 ATK opponent; a 2500 ATK opponent fails to destroy it the first time but a second battle destroys it; and two separate Terrarumian monsters each receive their own first-battle protection. Native Omega, negation of the Field Spell, and full turn-reset scenarios remain unverified. Current authored additions: 21 of 91; 70 scripts remain absent.

Terrarumian Growth (284636589) now has an authored Quick-Play and GY script with a shared once-per-turn count code. `node --no-warnings scripts/fresh_terrarumian_growth_place.cjs` verifies activation is not offered when only Growth itself could be destroyed, and in the valid case destroys a different Terrarumian card then places a Deck Pendulum in the Pendulum Zone; its GY effect is not offered again that turn. `node --no-warnings scripts/fresh_terrarumian_growth_set.cjs` verifies no GY activation without a different Terrarumian Spell/Trap, then banishes Growth as cost and Sets that target; a second hand copy cannot activate that turn. A destruction-replacement chain, full Pendulum Zones, and native Omega remain unverified. Current authored additions: 22 of 91; 69 scripts remain absent.

## First engine-driven differential scenarios — September 29

Run `node --no-warnings scripts/fresh_ccg_native_duels.cjs`.

The test constructs real engine cards and effects, reads CCG metadata from the database, progresses through engine prompts, activates the real Surfacing script, and checks resulting monster/graveyard counts. No Card, Duel, or Group APIs are mocked. A reference spell adapter runs Omega Krawler Spine's unchanged target/operation callbacks; only its trigger context is adapted to a Spell activation for comparison with Surfacing. A real continuous effect inspects the chain's targeting property.

Four scenarios compare the reference and Surfacing with an unprotected monster and a monster carrying native targeting protection. The unprotected cases must activate and destroy; protected-only cases must not offer activation. The chain must expose CARD_TARGET for the targeted destruction.

Reproduced and fixed in Surfacing and its generator:

- Spine copy legality counted matching monsters even when none could be targeted. Changed to IsExistingTarget. Axon's analogous check was also corrected against its Omega reference; Axon still needs a working engine scenario.
- Copied targeting branches did not set EFFECT_FLAG_CARD_TARGET. The selected branch now sets it for Spine, Axon and Ranvier, and clears it for non-targeting branches. Engine verification currently covers Spine only.

All four current scenarios pass. `--baseline` tests the committed Surfacing script without changing the working file and saves a separate report, enabling reproduction of both failures. Reports are in `output/fresh-ccg-september/`.

## Important limits and next work

Aquamarine Seagrass Zostera (284636661) now has an authored Trap-Monster script and candidate-database Trap-Monster stats (Aqua/WATER/Level 6/500/2300), which the generic Trap importer had encoded as zero. `node --no-warnings scripts/fresh_zostera_duels.cjs` passes three real-engine scenarios: it cannot activate on its Set turn without another Aquamarine card to discard; with a discard it Special Summons as a Trap Monster; when a Level 4-or-lower Aquamarine is in Deck it sends that monster and banishes a different GY Aquamarine. Its once-per-turn Quick Effect is also activated in the no-Deck-target case and banishes a targeted GY Aquamarine. The initial script incorrectly offered Set-turn activation for free; moving the discard payment onto the activation and gating Set-turn permission corrected it. The public query API exposes its combat stats but omits Type/Level for this Trap Monster; candidate DB validation checks those metadata fields, but native Omega verification remains open. Current authored additions: 15 of 91; 76 scripts remain absent.

Aquamarine Sand Clypeasteria (284636664) now has an authored script and staged prompt strings. `node --no-warnings scripts/fresh_clypeasteria_duels.cjs` passes three public-engine scenarios: hand effect is unavailable without an Aquamarine monster, succeeds with one, and its Special Summon trigger Fusion Summons Aquamarine Planktonites using two face-up banished Aquamarine materials returned to the GY. The Fusion scenario uses a narrowly scoped test adapter for the public core's missing `Duel.GetMustMaterial` and suppresses Planktonites' unrelated trigger, which calls unsupported `IsFaceupEx`; it retains the real Planktonites Fusion material recipe and Clypeasteria script. `node --no-warnings scripts/fresh_clypeasteria_revival.cjs` also passes two banished-from-GY cases: no revival offer without another Aquamarine in the GY; with one, the effect banishes that card as cost and revives Clypeasteria. Opponent-turn timing and native Omega compatibility remain unverified. Current authored additions: 16 of 91; 75 scripts remain absent.

Aquamarine Coral Krait (284636662) now has an authored Fusion procedure and staged prompt strings. `node --no-warnings scripts/fresh_coral_krait_send.cjs` passes 0/1/2-other-Fusion cases, verifying the Quick Effect's dynamic per-turn use limit and actual targeted sends to the GY. `node --no-warnings scripts/fresh_coral_krait_leave.cjs` passes three opponent-card removal cases: no replacement without a non-WATER opponent monster, replacement Fusion Summon with one, and no replacement when Coral Krait was not Fusion Summoned. The latter suite uses a test-only Summon Spell and opponent Trap, and retains Planktonites' actual Fusion material recipe while omitting its unrelated incompatible trigger. Full material-procedure duels, battle removal, costs, native Omega compatibility, and chain edge cases remain unverified. Current authored additions: 17 of 91; 74 scripts remain absent.

Aquamarine Bubble Colony (284636666) now has an authored distinct-name Fusion material procedure, GY-banish alternate Extra Deck Summon, unique-on-field limit, conditional battle indestructibility, and dynamic extra-attack field effect. `node --no-warnings scripts/fresh_bubble_colony_summon.cjs` passes four scenarios: one material and duplicate-name materials do not offer the alternate Summon; different names banish both cards and Summon; an existing Bubble Colony blocks a second copy. `node --no-warnings scripts/fresh_bubble_colony_fusion.cjs` passes three ordinary Fusion scenarios: duplicate names are rejected, different names work, and a duplicate plus a distinct third material still permits selecting a valid pair. The ordinary Fusion tests use a narrow test-only adapter for the public core's missing `Duel.GetMustMaterial` with no mandatory-material effects present. `node --no-warnings scripts/fresh_bubble_colony_battle.cjs` verifies that Bubble Colony is destroyed by battle alone but survives with another Aquamarine. `node --no-warnings scripts/fresh_bubble_colony_attacks.cjs` verifies a normal first attack with one or two eligible Level 8 Aquamarine Fusions, and a second attack when there are three differently named eligible Fusions. The latter assumes the printed cap cannot remove a monster's normal one attack; clarification has been requested. Native Omega compatibility and broader Fusion interactions remain unverified. Current authored additions: 18 of 91; 73 scripts remain absent.

Aquamarine Seafan Gorgonia (284636663) now has an authored script. Public-engine duels verify its hand Quick Effect requires another Aquamarine monster, Special Summons itself, and Sets Aquamarine Aquasanctuary from the Deck. A separate banish-from-GY test verifies Aquasanctuary search plus discard only while an Aquamarine is controlled. Two Extra Deck oath tests verify both directions: a prior non-Aquamarine Extra Deck Summon prevents Gorgonia's activation; after Gorgonia activates, non-Aquamarine Extra Deck Summons fail while Aquamarine ones succeed. Opponent-turn timing, chain interactions, Damage Step rules and native Omega remain open. Current authored additions: 14 of 91; 77 scripts remain absent.

All six newly added Aquamarine cards had empty setcodes in the staged candidate database, making their archetype searches, material checks and restrictions ineffective. They now have the existing Aquamarine setcode `0x0f3c`, with candidate-stage assertions for each row. Aquamarine Aquasanctuary (284636665) has an authored script. Two public-engine activation scenarios pass (Deck search available/unavailable); the available case searches then discards. A field duel driven by a test-only Fusion Summon verifies that it revives an Aquamarine monster from GY and that an opponent's attacker can target the Level 8 Fusion but not a Level 4 Aquamarine beside it. The duel exposed a field-effect condition callback bug (`tp` was nil); using the handler player fixed it. Damage Step exclusion, response to negation and native Omega compatibility remain unverified. Current authored additions: 13 of 91; 78 scripts remain absent.

Heavy-Armoured Ballista Bahariasaurus (244168521) now has an authored script. The importer previously silently encoded long-form Link Arrow names such as `up-left` as zero; five newer website Link cards were affected in the staged candidate DB. The importer now recognizes those directions, rejects unknown names, and verifies Bahariasaurus's four markers as `0x145` (`python scripts/test_link_arrows.py`). The public core cannot execute Omega's Link procedure (`Card.GetMustMaterial` absent) or its linked-zone field effect (`Card.GetLinkedZone` absent). The diagnostic `scripts/fresh_baharia_duels.cjs` records the former incompatibility. `node --no-warnings scripts/fresh_baharia_effects.cjs --omit-zone` removes only those two incompatible registrations in the test adapter, then Special Summons the card with a test-only effect. Its original triggered effect code passes: it Summons a Dinosaur to the opponent, destroys that monster and both horizontal neighboring Monster Zones, then later uses a Spell-card cost to revive and is banished when it leaves again. This is **not** Link-summon or zone-lock verification. The interpretation of "adjacent zones" as the left and right Monster Zones has been sent to the card author for confirmation. Current authored additions: 12 of 91; 79 scripts remain absent.

Mevalkagna, Eye of Zenith (244165675) now has an authored script and candidate prompt strings. Public-engine tests reject three GY monsters when two share the same Type/Attribute pair, and accept a legal three-target group across both GYs. They verify all three are banished, Mevalkagna is Summoned, and its resulting Type and Attribute include the banished cards' values. The same duel verifies a 1000 ATK reduction and permanent control of a matching opponent monster. A separate battle-through-End-Phase duel verifies that both battling monsters are destroyed and an uninvolved bystander survives. Another three-turn duel verifies that the controlled monster can attack and the Battle Phase cannot end before it does. The "if able" exception, conditional target loss, control redirection, and native Omega compatibility remain unverified. The interpretation of "different Types and/or Attributes" as unique Type/Attribute combinations has been sent to the card author for confirmation. Current authored additions: 11 of 91; 80 scripts remain absent.

Vanquish Soul Brawl (244161941) now has a script and official Vanquish Soul setcode 0x195 in the candidate DB. Five public-engine scenarios pass: same-Attribute targets do not permit activation; a different-Attribute VS monster from either GY or hand is Summoned and returns to hand in the End Phase; an effect from a non-VS card that removes an opponent's monster does not trigger Brawl; the corresponding VS-card effect does trigger Brawl and recycles exactly three cards. The latter two use a controlled test-only monster effect as the source, not a mocked Duel API. Native Omega and adverse interactions (redirection, chains, replacement, partial GY loss) remain to test. Current authored additions: 10 of 91; 81 scripts remain absent.

Vylon Observatory (238274862) now has an authored script and candidate-database prompts/setcode. The public engine passes two activation scenarios (`node --no-warnings scripts/fresh_vylon_observatory_duels.cjs`): with a Vylon Tuner in the Deck it Summons the Tuner, and without one the Field Spell still activates without Summoning. That test exposed and fixed an optional-effect bug: merely activating the Field Spell previously imposed the LIGHT-only restriction; the restriction is now attached to selecting the Tuner effect. The destroyed face-up Spell/Trap equip recovery and field-wide extra Synchro material are not yet engine-verified. The latter has no exact local Omega reference and requires native Omega validation. Current authored additions at that checkpoint: 9 of 91; 82 scripts remained absent.

Vylon Torus (238274860) and Vylon Chevron (238274857) are now authored. Torus passes three engine cases: no other Vylon card prevents its hand action; destroying a Vylon card Summons it; if the Deck holds both a Vylon monster and Spell, its Summon trigger searches both and discards one. Synchro material follow-up is unverified because public OCGCore lacks an Omega Synchro helper. Chevron passes two real Normal Summon scenarios: sending a separate Vylon cost Summons another from Deck, while sending itself from the field as cost also Summons from Deck and can trigger its GY equip effect. The latter is legal under the printed “Vylon Card from your hand or field” clause and is now an explicit regression scenario. Quick Synchro and full turn history restriction remain unverified. Current authored additions: 8 of 91; 83 scripts remain absent.

Vylon Glome (238274858) and Vylon Volt (238274859) are authored with signed-safe prompt strings and candidate DB setcodes. The Glome engine test reproduced a real equip bug: `Duel.Equip(...,false)` left the hand monster face-down, suppressing its equipped search. Using Omega's default equip call makes its hand equip and subsequent search work. The test also rejects a non-Vylon equip target and checks that the shared Union action is not offered again in that turn. The Volt engine test confirms that another Vylon is required in hand and, when present, both are Summoned before Volt equips a Vylon Union monster from the Deck to the other monster. GY Union paths, Glome's sent-to-GY trigger, negation and resets remain open. Current authored additions: 6 of 91; 85 scripts remain absent.

September 29 continuation: Vylon Eta GY equip and optional replacement have three passing engine scenarios (`node --no-warnings scripts/fresh_vylon_eta_duels.cjs`): no valid target, decline replacement, and accept replacement. The separate Synchro Summon test cannot complete in the public core because Omega's `procedure.lua` calls `Card.CheckSynchroMaterial`, which the public WASM card API lacks. Eta's Synchro search is still unverified; no test result is being counted for it.

Vylon's Aid (238274861) has a new Omega-pattern script for two targeted LIGHT summons with effect negation and a GY Equip Spell search. Its printed Constellar treatment is now encoded as setcode 0x53 in the candidate database; the source typo “alway treated” had prevented the generic parser from recognizing it. The public engine verifies that Aid cannot activate with only one LIGHT monster in the GY. Its two-target resolution test reaches the operation but hits the public core's `Passed invalid CHAININFO flag` on `CHAININFO_TARGET_CARDS=0x40`; Omega's unmodified c10971759 uses that exact operation pattern. Aid's successful summon and second effect need native Omega or another compatible runner before being counted as verified. Current authored additions: 4 of 91; 87 scripts remain absent.

The candidate build also caught that the remote cards were tagged `archetype: Existing`, so the generic importer assigned no Vylon setcode. All seven new Vylon rows now receive official Vylon setcode 0x30; Aid additionally receives Constellar 0x53. Candidate DB validation checks Eta and Aid setcodes and prompt strings. This is metadata preparation; five of the seven Vylon scripts still need to be authored.

Further engine coverage: `node --no-warnings scripts/fresh_machina_duels.cjs --megaframe` now passes four scenarios for actual hand-to-GY movement, the GY trigger, Level 10 send, revival, and the ensuing search (including another Megaframe). Native send-to-GY redirection banishes the Level 10 monster and correctly prevents revival. No controlled Machina and a Level 9 Deck substitute correctly suppress the GY trigger. The original four Trailblazer scenarios still pass. Tests use the staged database, hash tested inputs, and contain no Card/Duel mocks. Normal Summon paths, negation, full zones, repeat use and turn reset remain open.

Vylon Eta (238274863) has now been authored using Omega's Vylon Sigma Synchro procedure, Vylon Sphere equip pattern and Vylon Cube search pattern, with prompt strings included in the refreshed candidate DB. Syntax passes. Eta's search, GY equip, and optional destruction replacement still require engine tests and are NOT verified yet. Current authored additions: 3 of 91; 88 scripts remain absent.

Missing-card implementation checkpoint: Machina Megaframe (244163508) and Machina Trailblazer (244163509) now have Lua scripts and signed-safe effect-description overrides. Both parse successfully. `python scripts/stage_remote_omega.py` creates a separate candidate database from the pinned 711-card specification, applies the current community banlist flags, adds 91 records, and preserves 620 real-card records. The candidate has verified Machina setcodes and prompt strings for both new cards. This does not update the public database or publish anything.

`node --no-warnings scripts/fresh_machina_duels.cjs` uses that candidate's actual Trailblazer metadata and real engine objects. Four scenarios pass: EARTH/DARK Machine summon eligibility, WATER Machine and EARTH Warrior rejection, successful setting, and accepting/declining optional destruction. These do not yet cover every effect edge case, Normal Summon triggers, negation, full zones or shared once-per-turn use. Megaframe currently has syntax/reference review only and still needs engine scenarios. Of the 91 missing scripts at the pinned baseline, 2 have been authored and 89 remain; neither new card is fully certified yet.

September 29 continuation: Added eight real-engine restriction scenarios covering prior card/effect activations and response availability after Surfacing's restriction is registered, for World Legacy and non-World Legacy Spells. Reproduced a false block after activating a face-up non-World Legacy Spell effect. Both the activity counter and ongoing lock now distinguish EFFECT_TYPE_ACTIVATE, following Omega's c10024317.aclimit pattern. All 12 current scenarios pass. The future-action fixtures prove the action was available before the lock and require reaching a response window, preventing vacuous passes. Trap-specific cases, negation and end-turn reset still need coverage.

Scope update from the user: website-only cards must now be implemented, including the newer GitHub roster reported to contain 672 cards. The 16 local missing cards are no longer awaiting inclusion approval. Reconcile the remote roster without overwriting the unresolved local merge before establishing the final missing-card count.

Remote scope reconciled: fetched origin/main at b885aad510cc6cd685c00e43261881a5a6649fc6 without merging. It contains 711 distinct cards, with 91 missing local scripts and database entries. `python scripts/fresh_ccg_baseline.py --remote` saves full card specifications and pinned revision in `output/fresh-ccg-september/baseline-remote.json`. New implementations remain outstanding.

Community banlist update: applied the 11 requested changes effective September 15, 2026 to the Omega conf, source TCG records/ID mapping, custom Scarstech Deployment source flag, and public card/banlist datasets. Website heading now shows the effective date from `src/data/banlist-update.json`; Omega regeneration reads that same date and recognizes explicit TCG passcodes. `python scripts/test_september_banlist.py` passes three read-only tests checking all 11 statuses, both custom merge views, and preservation of all unrelated restrictions. The existing merge conflicts still prevent a full website build; changes are local, not published or repackaged into the installer.

- This is EDOPro's public OCGCore WASM with the local Omega Lua support files, NOT the native Omega executable. Native Omega confirmation remains outstanding.
- The public core lacks Omega's Card.IsSpellTrap method. Continuing into Surfacing's later GY availability scan exposed this infrastructure mismatch. The current targeted scenarios stop at chain end and do not claim to test the GY effect. No compatibility shim or production behavior was added to hide the mismatch.
- Copied target redirection, non-targeting branches, whole-turn activation restrictions, GY recovery, and all other cards still require fresh scenario coverage.
- The pinned remote scope contains 711 website cards (the unresolved local merge contains 636); none has complete fresh all-effects verification yet. Passing focused scenarios do not constitute fully verified cards.
- Roster/package merge conflicts and release packaging are untouched. Nothing has been published.
